# S4VY: Segment Anything in Feed-Forward 4D Visual Geometry

Jingdong Zhang<sup>1</sup>, Xin Li<sup>1</sup>, Jan Kautz<sup>2</sup>, Wenping Wang<sup>1</sup>, Chris Choy<sup>2</sup> <sup>1</sup>Texas A&M Universit<sub>y,</sub> Colle<sub>g</sub>e Station<sub>,</sub> <sup>2</sup>NVIDIA

![](images/6915e59bb9c00de338ce58e7c7f1bce1a0f86b49af42edfba9c33c813ecfd837.jpg)  
Figure 1: Overview of S4VY. Left: Given a set of RGB observations, S4VY predicts a consistent 4D mask for every instance in the scene. Right: Any instance can be grounded via either a natural-language prompt or an ima<sub>g</sub>e-level <sub>p</sub>oint/box <sub>p</sub>rom<sub>p</sub>t.

## Abstract

Accurate instance segmentation in dynamic scenes is important for downstream applications such as robotics and autonomous driving. Existing Segment Anything models operate primarily on 2D image or video masks and preserve identity through sequential memory, while promptable 4D instance segmentation built upon feed-forward visual geometry remains underexplored. We introduce S4VY, a Segment Anything model built on feed-forward 4D visual geometry. From a set of RGB observations, S4VY transforms shared visual-geometric features into an exhaustive set of class-agnostic 4D instance masks through a space-time query decoder, with each persistent object query binding one entity across all observations. This representation supports prompt-independent segmentation as well as point- and boxconditioned selection, without requiring a seed mask or temporal ordering. We further develop an agentic harness for natural-language grounding in the large observation space of a 4D scene. Active tree search identifies relevant frames without scanning every fixed window; a dual-stream grounder combines fine-grained VLM visual priors with geometry-consistent instance features through complementary bounding-box prediction and object-query matching; and an independent critic selects the final 4D instance mask from their predictions. Extensive experiments demonstrate state-of-the-art 4D instance segmentation and strong language-guided grounding performance under a unified evaluation spanning static and dynamic scenes.

## Segment Anything models.

Video extensions either attach trackin<sub>g</sub> or tem<sub>p</sub>oral memor<sub>y</sub> to ima<sub>g</sub>e masks (Chen<sub>g</sub> et al., 2023; Rajič et al., 2025; Ravi et al., 2025; Yan<sub>g</sub> et al., 2023a), with SAM 3 (Carion et al., 2026) further su<sub>pp</sub>ortin<sub>g</sub> text and exem<sub>p</sub>lar conce<sub>p</sub>ts, or re<sub>p</sub>resent instances with <sub>q</sub>ueries shared across a cli<sub>p</sub> (Chen<sub>g</sub> et al., 2021; Wu et al., 2022b); both remain defined over 2D ima<sub>g</sub>e or video re<sub>p</sub>resentations. ## 3.2. Segmentation design

## Space-Time Query Decoder.

Our Space-Time Query Decoder exploits the rich cross-view and temporal correspondences encoded by feedf<sub>orwar</sub>d <sub>v</sub>i<sub>sua</sub>l <sub>geome</sub>t<sub>ry</sub> t<sub>o recover pers</sub>i<sub>s</sub>t<sub>en</sub>t 4D i<sub>ns</sub>t<sub>ances.</sub> W<sub>e</sub> f<sub>ormu</sub>l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub> thi<sub>s process as en</sub>d<sub>-</sub>t<sub>o-en</sub>d <sub>se</sub>t <sub>p</sub>rediction followin<sub>g</sub> Mask2Former (Chen<sub>g</sub> et al., 2022), avoidin<sub>g</sub> the <sub>p</sub>ost-clusterin<sub>g</sub> identit<sub>y</sub> association used b<sub>y</sub> the IGGT series (Li et al., 2025a; Zou et al., 2026). A set of <sub>g</sub>lobal learnable object <sub>q</sub>ueries $\{ \mathbf { q } _ { j } \}$ jointly attends to the LoRA-ada<sub>p</sub>ted (Hu et al., 2021) contextualized <sub>p</sub>atch tokens $\{ \mathbf { z } _ { i } ^ { F } \} _ { i = 1 } ^ { T }$ . The Space-Time Query D<sub>e</sub>c<sub>o</sub>d<sub>e</sub>r $f _ { \mathrm { S T } }$ <sub>pre</sub>di<sub>c</sub>t<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e comp</sub>l<sub>e</sub>t<sub>e</sub> 4D <sub>mas</sub>k <sub>o</sub>f <sub>eac</sub>h <sub>query as</sub>

$$
\begin{array} { r } { \hat { \bf M } _ { j } = f _ { \mathrm { S T } } \big ( { \bf q } _ { j } , \{ { \bf z } _ { i } ^ { F } \} \big ) . } \end{array}\tag{2}
$$

During training, bipartite matching assigns each ground-truth 4D instance to a unique learnable object query, with unmatched predictions assigned to the no-object class.

## 4.1. Grounding components

## Grounder.

Vision-Lan<sub>g</sub>ua<sub>g</sub>e Models (VLMs) <sub>p</sub>rovide stron<sub>g</sub> s<sub>p</sub>atial <sub>p</sub>riors for <sub>g</sub>roundin<sub>g</sub> referred objects in individual ima<sub>g</sub>es, <sub>ye</sub>t th<sub>e</sub>i<sub>r</sub> <sub>na</sub>ti<sub>ve</sub> RGB i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>er</sub>f<sub>ace</sub> d<sub>oes</sub> <sub>no</sub>t <sub>exp</sub>l<sub>o</sub>it th<sub>e</sub> <sub>cross-v</sub>i<sub>ew</sub> <sub>cons</sub>i<sub>s</sub>t<sub>ency</sub> <sub>enco</sub>d<sub>e</sub>d b<sub>y</sub> f<sub>ee</sub>d<sub>-</sub>f<sub>orwar</sub>d <sub>v</sub>i<sub>sua</sub>l <sub>g</sub>eometr<sub>y</sub>. The backbone re<sub>g</sub>ister tokens form a com<sub>p</sub>act, lan<sub>g</sub>ua<sub>g</sub>e-conditioned scene re<sub>p</sub>resentation (Wan<sub>g</sub> et al., 2026a). Su<sub>pp</sub>l<sub>y</sub>in<sub>g</sub> all <sub>p</sub>atch tokens from multi<sub>p</sub>le observations would introduce <sub>p</sub>rohibitive context redundancy; we therefore inject the per-frame camera and register tokens as compact geometric context. We further su<sub>p</sub>ervise the <sub>g</sub>rounder with a scene-<sub>g</sub>eometr<sub>y</sub>-<sub>g</sub>uided chain of thou<sub>g</sub>ht (CoT) (Wei et al., 2022) distilled from visual and metric scene cues, including object size, height, and inter-object distance, assisting scene <sub>reason</sub>i<sub>ng an</sub>d <sub>query an</sub>d b<sub>ox pre</sub>di<sub>c</sub>ti<sub>ons.</sub>

Algorithm 1 Active Tree Search   
Input: observations $\mathcal { T } = \{ I _ { i } \} _ { i = 1 } ^ { T } ,$ ex<sub>p</sub>ress<sup>i</sup>on $x ,$ <sub>w</sub>i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>ow</sub> <sub>s</sub>i<sub>ze</sub> $N _ { \mathrm { w } } ,$ and maximum depth �

1: $\mathcal { E }  \mathtt { S e a R C H } ( [ 1 , T ] , 0 ) .$   
2: $( \hat { \mathbf { M } } ^ { q } , \hat { \mathbf { M } } ^ { p } ) \gets$ aggregate and decode both prediction streams in ℰ.   
3: $\hat { \mathbf { M } } ^ { * } \gets f _ { \mathrm { C } } ( x , \hat { \mathbf { M } } ^ { q } , \hat { \mathbf { M } } ^ { p } ) ;$ ; return $\hat { \mathbf { M } } ^ { * }$   
function Search $( [ a , b ] , d )$   
4: Uniforml<sub>y</sub> sam<sub>p</sub>le $N _ { \mathrm { w } }$ indices � from $[ a , b ] ;$ <sub>use ever</sub> i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>ex</sub> if $b - a + 1 \leq N _ { \mathrm { w } } .$   
5: $\{ ( \widehat { \mathbf { q } } _ { i } , \widehat { p } _ { i } ) \} _ { i \in S }  f _ { \mathrm { G } } ( x , \{ I _ { i } \} _ { i \in S } ) ;$ <sub>a</sub>dd th<sub>e pre</sub>di<sub>c</sub>ti<sub>ons</sub> t<sub>o</sub> $\varepsilon .$   
6: if the indices in � are adjacent, or $d = D ,$ then return ℰ.   
7: $P  \{ i \in S \mid \hat { p } _ { i } \neq \emptyset \} ; \mathcal { C }  \mathrm { E x p A N D } ( [ a , b ] , S , P , d ) .$   
8: for each $C \in { \mathcal { C } } , { \mathrm { s e t } } { \mathcal { E } } \gets { \mathcal { E } } \cup { \mathrm { S E A R C H } } ( C , d + 1 ) .$   
9: return ℰ.   
function Expand $( [ a , b ] , S , P , d )$

in �.

## Self-distillation.

## Optimization.

After Hun<sub>g</sub>arian matchin<sub>g</sub> (Carion et al., 2020; Chen<sub>g</sub> et al., 2022), the se<sub>g</sub>menter is trained with object classification, binar<sub>y</sub> cross-entro<sub>py</sub>, and soft Dice losses (Milletari et al., 2016):

$$
\mathcal { L } _ { \mathrm { s e g } } = \mathcal { L } _ { \mathrm { o b j } } + \mathcal { L } _ { \mathrm { B C E } } + \mathcal { L } _ { \mathrm { D i c e } } .\tag{7}
$$

## Baselines.

For se<sub>g</sub>mentation, we com<sub>p</sub>are lifted 3D se<sub>g</sub>mentation (Yan<sub>g</sub> et al., 2023b), ima<sub>g</sub>e/video s<sub>y</sub>stems (Chen<sub>g</sub> et al., 2023; Li et al., 2024b; Ravi et al., 2025; Wu et al., 2024), and feed-forward visual-<sub>g</sub>eometr<sub>y</sub> methods (Gon<sub>g</sub> et al., 2026; Li et al., 2025a; Qu et al., 2026; Zou et al., 2026; Zust et al., 2025). Oficial check<sub>p</sub>oints receive th<sub>e same o</sub>b<sub>serva</sub>ti<sub>ons w</sub>ith<sub>ou</sub>t <sub>seman</sub>ti<sub>c</sub> l<sub>a</sub>b<sub>e</sub>l<sub>s,</sub> t<sub>arge</sub>t <sub>promp</sub>t<sub>s, or</sub> fi<sub>rs</sub>t<sub>-</sub>f<sub>rame groun</sub>d t<sub>ru</sub>th<sub>.</sub> SAM 2 <sub>uses</sub> <sub>au</sub>t<sub>oma</sub>ti<sub>c mas</sub>k di<sub>scover w</sub>ith <sub>er</sub>i<sub>o</sub>di<sub>c re-</sub>di<sub>scover , w</sub>hil<sub>e memor -</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>ens</sub>i<sub>ve</sub> b<sub>ase</sub>li<sub>nes re</sub>t<sub>a</sub>i<sub>n a</sub>ll <sub>o</sub>b<sub>serva</sub>ti<sub>ons</sub> th<sub>rou</sub> h f<sub>u</sub>ll<sub>-se uence or s</sub>t<sub>ream</sub>i<sub>n</sub> i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>erence.</sub> G<sub>roun</sub>di<sub>n</sub> b<sub>ase</sub>li<sub>nes cover</sub> VLM<sub>–se men</sub>t<sub>er an</sub>d 3D<sub>- ro osa</sub>l <sub>p</sub>i<sub>p</sub>elines (Drozdov et al., 2026; Ravi et al., 2025; Xu et al., 2024), end-to-end ima<sub>g</sub>e/video referrin<sub>g</sub> models (Carion et al., 2026; Yuan et al., 2026a), and multi-view <sub>g</sub>eometr<sub>y</sub>–lan<sub>g</sub>ua<sub>g</sub>e ali<sub>g</sub>nment (Wu et al., 2026a). ## Implementation details.

The se<sub>g</sub>menter initializes from the VGGT-Ω-1B check<sub>p</sub>oint at 512-<sub>p</sub>ixel resolution. We use rank-256 LoRA (Hu et al., 2021) ada<sub>p</sub>ters and train the se<sub>g</sub>menter for 50,000 ste<sub>p</sub>s. The Grounder initializes from Qwen3-VL-4B (Bai et al., 2025), uses ei<sub>g</sub>ht-observation windows durin<sub>g</sub> trainin<sub>g</sub> and inference, and is trained on a<sub>pp</sub>roximatel<sub>y</sub> 80k expressions. The Critic initializes from Qwen3-VL-2B and is trained separately on paired mask candidates. R<sub>epor</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>promp</sub>ti<sub>ng resu</sub>lt<sub>s use</sub> th<sub>e separa</sub>t<sub>e</sub>l<sub>y</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>ne</sub>d P<sub>romp</sub>t E<sub>nco</sub>d<sub>er.</sub> Additi<sub>ona</sub>l t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng an</sub>d b<sub>ase</sub>li<sub>ne se</sub>tti<sub>ngs</sub> are <sub>g</sub>iven in Sec. B.

In <sub>p</sub>anel (c), several b<sub>as</sub>k<sub>e</sub>tb<sub>a</sub>ll <sub>p</sub>l<sub>ayers</sub> h<sub>ave s</sub>i<sub>m</sub>il<sub>ar appearances an</sub>d <sub>repea</sub>t<sub>e</sub>dl<sub>y occ</sub>l<sub>u</sub>d<sub>e one ano</sub>th<sub>er</sub> d<sub>ur</sub>i<sub>ng rap</sub>id <sub>mo</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub> Th<sub>e</sub> <sub>purp</sub>l<sub>e p</sub>l<sub>ayer</sub>’<sub>s</sub> id<sub>en</sub>tit<sub>y rema</sub>i<sub>ns s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> th<sub>roug</sub>h th<sub>e s</sub>h<sub>o</sub>t<sub>, w</sub>hil<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e</sub> b<sub>a</sub>ll i<sub>s s</sub>till t<sub>rac</sub>k<sub>e</sub>d <sub>a</sub>ft<sub>er possess</sub>i<sub>on passes</sub> t<sub>o</sub> the red <sub>p</sub>la<sub>y</sub>er. ## A. Additional Method Details

## A.1. Space-Time Query Decoder

As illustrated in Fi<sub>g</sub>. 3(b), the S<sub>p</sub>ace-Time Quer<sub>y</sub> Decoder se<sub>p</sub>arates <sub>g</sub>lobal instance reasonin<sub>g</sub> from dense mask <sub>pre</sub>di<sub>c</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub> Th<sub>e con</sub>t<sub>ex</sub>t<sub>ua</sub>li<sub>ze</sub>d <sub>pa</sub>t<sub>c</sub>h t<sub>o</sub>k<sub>ens</sub> $\{ \mathbf { z } _ { i } ^ { F } \} _ { i = } ^ { T }$ follow two complementary paths. A projection $g _ { \mathrm { m e m } }$ maps them to a joint decoder memory

$$
\mathbf { Z } = { \mathrm { C o n c a t } } _ { i = 1 } ^ { T } g _ { \mathrm { { m e m } } } \left( \mathbf { z } _ { i } ^ { F } \right) ,\tag{9}
$$

## A.3. Training Data Details

Tab. 1 details the 19 sources summarized in Fi<sub>g</sub>. 5(c). The re<sub>p</sub>orted counts refer to the subsets sam<sub>p</sub>led for trainin rather than the full source releases. In articular, we sam le 5,127 trajectories from RealEstate10K, 4<sub>,</sub>635 cli<sub>p</sub>s from SA-V<sub>,</sub> and 9<sub>,</sub>649 scenes from DL3DV. The resultin<sub>g</sub> cor<sub>p</sub>us contains 33<sub>,</sub>220 scenes<sub>,</sub> sub-scenes<sub>,</sub> <sub>or v</sub>id<sub>eo c</sub>li<sub>ps an</sub>d 6<sub>,</sub>052<sub>,</sub>440 RGB <sub>o</sub>b<sub>serva</sub>ti<sub>ons spann</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>oor, ou</sub>td<sub>oor, an</sub>d <sub>m</sub>i<sub>xe</sub>d <sub>env</sub>i<sub>ronmen</sub>t<sub>s as we</sub>ll <sub>as</sub> <sub>rea</sub>l <sub>an</sub>d <sub>syn</sub>th<sub>e</sub>ti<sub>c</sub> i<sub>magery.</sub>

Th<sub>e ex</sub>h<sub>aus</sub>ti<sub>ve su</sub>b<sub>se</sub>t <sub>prov</sub>id<sub>es</sub> th<sub>e se</sub>t<sub>-</sub>l<sub>eve</sub>l <sub>superv</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>on requ</sub>i<sub>re</sub>d t<sub>o</sub> di<sub>scover every anno</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d i<sub>ns</sub>t<sub>ance.</sub> Its static com<sub>p</sub>onent combines <sub>g</sub>eometr<sub>y</sub>-consistent s<sub>y</sub>nthetic views from Infini<sub>g</sub>en (Raistrick et al., 2023), real indoor ca<sub>p</sub>tures from ScanNet++ (Yeshwanth et al., 2023), and lar<sub>g</sub>e-scale camera trajectories from RealEstate10K (Zhou et al., 2018). ## Active Tree Search.

ATS is defined over the supplied observation order; adjacency therefore refers to neighboring input observations, which may follow a static scan trajectory or a dynamic sequence. The complete recursive traversal is given in Al<sub>g</sub>orithm 1.

## Grounding data construction.

Grounder data. We combine the human referring annotations of ScanRefer (Chen et al., 2020), MeViS (Ding et al., 2023a), and Ref-DAVIS (Khoreva et al., 2018) with descri<sub>p</sub>tions <sub>g</sub>enerated for ScanNet++ (Yeshwanth et al. 2023) and LVOS (Hon et al. 2023) which do not rovide referrin text. For ScanNet++ we use <sub>g</sub>round-truth 3D instances and their semantic labels as class anchors. Qwen3-VL-32B (Bai et al., 2025) receives th<sub>e mos</sub>t <sub>v</sub>i<sub>s</sub>ibl<sub>e</sub> RGB <sub>o</sub>b<sub>serva</sub>ti<sub>on an</sub>d <sub>up</sub> t<sub>o</sub> t<sub>wo spa</sub>ti<sub>a</sub>ll<sub>y separa</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>con</sub>t<sub>ex</sub>t <sub>o</sub>b<sub>serva</sub>ti<sub>ons</sub> t<sub>o genera</sub>t<sub>e up</sub> t<sub>o</sub> fi<sub>ve</sub> descriptions per instance. Quality control filters refusals, regenerates descriptions that omit the class anchor, checks hallucinations, contradictions, and neighboring-object errors with a VLM, and removes exact duplicates. For LVOS, we use GPT-5.6 (O<sub>p</sub>enAI, 2026) to <sub>g</sub>enerate referrin<sub>g</sub>-ex<sub>p</sub>ression annotations for its video se<sub>q</sub>uences. Each description is associated with its annotated instance or track and matched to a persistent object query by <sub>mas</sub>k <sub>over</sub>l<sub>ap over</sub> th<sub>e ava</sub>il<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e o</sub>b<sub>serva</sub>ti<sub>ons.</sub> F<sub>or</sub> S<sub>can</sub>N<sub>e</sub>t++ <sub>an</sub>d th<sub>e v</sub>id<sub>eo sources, we</sub> di<sub>scar</sub>d t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng pa</sub>i<sub>rs</sub> whose predicted masks have 2D mask IoU below 0.4 with the annotations, preventing incomplete or fragmented <sub>pre</sub>di<sub>c</sub>ti<sub>ons</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>ro</sub>d<sub>uc</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> i<sub>ncorrec</sub>t <sub>groun</sub>di<sub>ng correspon</sub>d<sub>ences.</sub> Aft<sub>er</sub> t<sub>arge</sub>t filt<sub>er</sub>i<sub>ng an</sub>d <sub>source</sub> b<sub>a</sub>l<sub>anc</sub>i<sub>ng,</sub> the trainin loader contains 79 856 records from 3 065 scenes or videos: 30 352 from ScanRefer 31 764 from ScanNet++<sub>,</sub> 16<sub>,</sub>888 from MeViS<sub>,</sub> 435 from LVOS<sub>,</sub> and 417 from Ref-DAVIS.

G<sub>eome</sub>t<sub>ry-gu</sub>id<sub>e</sub>d C<sub>o</sub>T <sub>superv</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>on</sub> i<sub>s genera</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d f<sub>or</sub> th<sub>e</sub> k<sub>nown re</sub>f<sub>eren</sub>t i<sub>n a separa</sub>t<sub>e s</sub>t<sub>age.</sub> F<sub>or v</sub>id<sub>eo</sub> d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>ase</sub>t<sub>s</sub> without 3D/4D <sub>g</sub>round truth, the visual <sub>g</sub>eometr<sub>y</sub> backbone (Wan<sub>g</sub> et al., 2026a) reconstructs de<sub>p</sub>th and camera geometry directly from the RGB observations. Back-projecting the ground-truth masks through this geometry yields an object-centric 4D point track, whose per-observation centroids and extents describe the referent’s location, motion, and spatial relations to nearby instances. Static-scene geometry further provides metric object extent, height above the floor, and metric inter-object distances. Since monocular video reconstruction has <sub>ne</sub>ith<sub>er an a</sub>b<sub>so</sub>l<sub>u</sub>t<sub>e sca</sub>l<sub>e nor a grav</sub>it<sub>y-a</sub>li<sub>gne</sub>d f<sub>rame,</sub> it<sub>s</sub> f<sub>ac</sub>t<sub>s</sub> i<sub>ns</sub>t<sub>ea</sub>d <sub>use</sub> t<sub>arge</sub>t <sub>v</sub>i<sub>s</sub>ibilit<sub>y, s</sub>i<sub>ze-norma</sub>li<sub>ze</sub>d motion, relative object size and distance, and variation in camera distance. Qwen3-VL-235B receives these f<sub>ac</sub>t<sub>s,</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>re</sub>f<sub>err</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> <sub>express</sub>i<sub>on,</sub> <sub>up</sub> t<sub>o</sub> f<sub>our</sub> <sub>un</sub>if<sub>orm</sub>l<sub>y</sub> <sub>samp</sub>l<sub>e</sub>d RGB <sub>o</sub>b<sub>serva</sub>ti<sub>ons,</sub> <sub>an</sub>d th<sub>e</sub> k<sub>nown</sub> t<sub>arge</sub>t<sub>,</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>pro</sub>d<sub>uces a s</sub>h<sub>or</sub>t <sub>ra</sub>ti<sub>ona</sub>l<sub>e</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>mus</sub>t <sub>com</sub>bi<sub>ne an appearance cue w</sub>ith <sub>a</sub>t l<sub>eas</sub>t <sub>one geome</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>c or spa</sub>ti<sub>o</sub>t<sub>empora</sub>l <sub>cue w</sub>ith<sub>ou</sub>t <sub>revea</sub>li<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>arge</sub>t i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>ex.</sub>

Critic data. We discard pairs in which neither candidate reaches 0.5 and subsample near-ties, leaving 4,005 comparisons; this prevents jointly f<sub>a</sub>il<sub>e</sub>d <sub>or am</sub>bi<sub>guous pre</sub>di<sub>c</sub>ti<sub>ons</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>ro</sub>d<sub>uc</sub>i<sub>ng no</sub>i<sub>sy compara</sub>ti<sub>ve superv</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>on.</sub>

## B. Experimental Details

## B.1. Segmenter and Prompt Encoder Training

## Self-distillation with incomplete annotations.

After Hungarian matching, exhaustive set prediction supervises every unmatched query as no-object. Applied directly to partial annotations, this objective would suppress unlabelled instances, while unlabelled sequences <sub>prov</sub>id<sub>e no pos</sub>iti<sub>ve</sub> t<sub>arge</sub>t<sub>s.</sub> W<sub>e</sub> th<sub>ere</sub>f<sub>ore</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n a</sub> t<sub>eac</sub>h<sub>er on</sub> th<sub>e ex</sub>h<sub>aus</sub>ti<sub>ve sources an</sub>d <sub>use</sub> it t<sub>o</sub> d<sub>ens</sub>if<sub>y</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>rema</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>a.</sub> O<sub>r</sub>i<sub>g</sub>i<sub>na</sub>l <sub>mas</sub>kl<sub>e</sub>t<sub>s</sub> <sub>are</sub> <sub>augmen</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>w</sub>ith <sub>non-over</sub>l<sub>app</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> t<sub>eac</sub>h<sub>er</sub> <sub>pre</sub>di<sub>c</sub>ti<sub>ons</sub> f<sub>or</sub> <sub>par</sub>ti<sub>a</sub>ll<sub>y</sub> <sub>anno</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>samp</sub>l<sub>es, w</sub>h<sub>ereas un</sub>l<sub>a</sub>b<sub>e</sub>ll<sub>e</sub>d <sub>samp</sub>l<sub>es are superv</sub>i<sub>se</sub>d <sub>en</sub>ti<sub>re</sub>l<sub>y</sub> b<sub>y</sub> th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>eac</sub>h<sub>er.</sub> Th<sub>e s</sub>h<sub>are</sub>d t<sub>eac</sub>h<sub>er–</sub> <sub>s</sub>t<sub>u</sub>d<sub>en</sub>t <sub>arc</sub>hit<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>ure</sub> th<sub>us</sub> t<sub>rans</sub>f<sub>ers ex</sub>h<sub>aus</sub>ti<sub>ve superv</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>on</sub> t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e</sub> b<sub>roa</sub>d<sub>er corpus</sub> th<sub>roug</sub>h <sub>se</sub>lf<sub>-</sub>di<sub>s</sub>till<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub>

Teacher predictions above 0.7 object probability are retained, regions between 0.3 and 0.7 are ignored, and the no-object loss is preserved elsewhere. Human annotations take precedence; overlapping teacher masks <sub>are</sub> di<sub>scar</sub>d<sub>e</sub>d<sub>, an</sub>d <sub>eac</sub>h <sub>re</sub>t<sub>a</sub>i<sub>ne</sub>d <sub>pseu</sub>d<sub>o-mas</sub>kl<sub>e</sub>t i<sub>s we</sub>i<sub>g</sub>ht<sub>e</sub>d b<sub>y</sub> h<sub>a</sub>lf it<sub>s</sub> t<sub>eac</sub>h<sub>er con</sub>fid<sub>ence</sub> b<sub>e</sub>f<sub>ore</sub> H<sub>ungar</sub>i<sub>an</sub> <sub>ma</sub>t<sub>c</sub>hi<sub>ng.</sub> Thi<sub>s</sub> th<sub>ree-</sub>b<sub>an</sub>d <sub>superv</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>on</sub> d<sub>ens</sub>ifi<sub>es par</sub>ti<sub>a</sub>l l<sub>a</sub>b<sub>e</sub>l<sub>s w</sub>ith<sub>ou</sub>t <sub>ass</sub>i<sub>gn</sub>i<sub>ng uncer</sub>t<sub>a</sub>i<sub>n pre</sub>di<sub>c</sub>ti<sub>ons</sub> t<sub>o e</sub>ith<sub>er</sub> f<sub>oregroun</sub>d <sub>or</sub> b<sub>ac</sub>k<sub>groun</sub>d<sub>.</sub>

The segmenter is optimized for 50,000 steps from the same 1B visual geometry checkpoint using 8–16 randomly sampled observations at 512-pixel resolution. AdamW uses a global batch size of 64, weight decay 0.05, gradient clipping at 1.0, and 5% linear warm-up followed by cosine decay. Peak learning rates are $2 . 8 3 \times 1 0 ^ { - 5 }$ for rank-256 LoRA adapters and $5 . 6 6 \times 1 0 ^ { - 4 }$ for the Space-Time Query Decoder and mask heads. The number for learnable object queries is set as 400. Object classification, BCE, and Dice losses have weights 2, 5, and 5, with a no-object weight of 0.1. The exhaustive to incomplete sampling ratio increases linearly from 1:10 to 2:1. Since incomplete samples rely on teacher pseudo-masklets, this curriculum uses their diversity to <sub>acce</sub>l<sub>era</sub>t<sub>e ear</sub>l<sub>y convergence,</sub> th<sub>en progress</sub>i<sub>ve</sub>l<sub>y</sub> f<sub>avors ex</sub>h<sub>aus</sub>ti<sub>ve anno</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ons</sub> t<sub>o re</sub>d<sub>uce</sub> th<sub>e</sub> i<sub>n</sub>fl<sub>uence o</sub>f <sub>no</sub>i<sub>sy</sub> labels. Training uses 64 NVIDIA A100 GPUs.

We fully fine-tune Qwen3-VL-4B for 4 epochs, corresponding to approximately 5,000 optimizer updates on eight GPUs. AdamW uses an efective batch size of 64, weight decay 0.01, and peak learning rates of $8 \times 1 0 ^ { - 6 }$ f<sub>o</sub>r th<sub>e</sub> VLM and $1 0 ^ { - 4 }$ for the newly initialized projection and prediction heads. We apply 300 warm-up steps followed by cosine decay, bfloat16 training, and gradient clipping at 1.0. The query-classification and autoregressive language-modeling losses in Eq. (6) are equally <sub>we</sub>i<sub>g</sub>ht<sub>e</sub>d<sub>.</sub>

## Critic.

The 4,005 candidate pairs constructed in Sec. A.4 are shufled during training, and their presentation order is randomized on every visit to remove branch-position bias. Near-tie examples constitute 30% of the training pairs, exposing the Critic to both clear and ambiguous comparisons. We fully fine-tune Qwen3-VL-2B with its scalar readout for three epochs on four GPUs. AdamW uses an efective batch size of 32, a learning rate of $5 \times 1 0 ^ { - 6 }$ , weight decay 0.01, bfloat16 precision, and gradient clipping at 1.0.

## Language-grounding baselines.

VLM+SAM 2 uses Qwen3-VL-30B-A3B-Instruct, and Sa2VA uses its released 8B model. ## B.4. Benchmarks and Metrics

## Benchmarks.

For class-a<sub>g</sub>nostic se<sub>g</sub>mentation, we use the ScanNet settin<sub>g</sub> from IGGT (Li et al., 2025a), DAVIS 2017, LVOS, and VIPSeg, comprising ten static multi-view clips, 30 short videos, 50 long-term videos, and 343 panoptic videos, res<sub>p</sub>ectivel<sub>y</sub>. Followin<sub>g</sub> (Li et al., 2025a; Zou et al., 2026), each ScanNet cli<sub>p</sub> contains 8 sam<sub>p</sub>led observations, while each LVOS video is uniformly sampled to 100 observations. For the separate robustness evaluation, each video produces three sparse subsets by sampling 16 observations without replacement from its first 32 observations, last 32 observations, and full temporal extent, respectively; the sampled observations retain their chronological order, and each subset contains annotated foreground. The shufle setting randomly permutes exactly the same observations and their annotations. For the LVOS hard setting, farthest-point sampling over normalized RGB thumbnail descriptors selects 16 appearance-diverse observations across the full <sub>v</sub>id<sub>eo; a</sub>t l<sub>eas</sub>t f<sub>our con</sub>t<sub>a</sub>i<sub>n anno</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d f<sub>ore roun</sub>d <sub>w</sub>h<sub>en ava</sub>il<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e, a</sub>ft<sub>er w</sub>hi<sub>c</sub>h th<sub>e</sub>i<sub>r or</sub>d<sub>er</sub> i<sub>s s</sub>h<sub>u</sub>fl<sub>e</sub>d<sub>.</sub> Additi<sub>ona</sub>l evaluations further cover two zero-shot egocentric benchmarks: 48 HOI4D (Liu et al., 2022) clips and 42 EPIC-KITCHENS VISOR (Darkhalil et al., 2022) cli<sub>p</sub>s, as well as 9 KITTI-STEP (Weber et al., 2021) se<sub>q</sub>uences for driving scenes. For language grounding, we sample 700 ScanRefer queries from 121 validation scenes and hold out 80 ScanNet++ scenes with 3,320 ex ressions. The video evaluation contains all 122 Ref-DAVIS expressions and 616 single-referent MeViS expressions from 47 videos.

## C. Additional Results and Discussion

## C.1. Eficiency and Additional Ablations

## Segmentation eficiency.
